# Financial Autonomy — current project state

## Operating mode (2026-11-07)

**WEB development is user-directed.** The hourly Web Premium automation was paused at the user's request; no autonomous WEB product work is authorized until the user selects it. Keep the independent iOS automation and repository untouched. Continue in the same GitHub repositories, preserving AGENTS.md, RELEASE_PROCESS.md, DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md, IMPLEMENTATION_QUEUE.md, GitHub issues and prior history.

## Verified handoff baseline

- WEB preview repository: `nikopol000/financial-autonomy-preview`, branch `main`.
- Active version in `index.html` at handoff: **18.74**. This is a version observation, not a claim that every feature is end-to-end verified.
- Public preview: https://financial-autonomy-preview.vercel.app/ . Last inspected production deployment was READY for WEB 18.74; browser/Safari interaction was not independently verified in this handoff.
- Existing unresolved issues: **#29** reset integrity (critical, four separate safety gates; acceptance test not confirmed green), **#35** encrypted first-save recovery, **#4** stale-tab/data integrity, **#14** budget perspectives (DISCOVERY ONLY; financial contract not approved), **#85** mobile accessibility/zoom. Consult live issues and queue for current details.
- `IMPLEMENTATION_QUEUE.md` contains extensive chronological checkpoints and must be preserved, not replaced or summarized destructively.
- No application bundle, financial formula, financial data, diagnostic export, release metadata or iOS code was changed during this operating-mode handoff. Documentation-only commits do not trigger a version bump.

## First unfinished action / next user decision

The operating-mode handoff is complete after the documentation updates are committed and verified. **Wait for the user to choose the next WEB work item** (e.g. dashboard premium design, budget perspectives analysis, or a critical safety issue). On the next development request, read current GitHub HEAD and all continuity files; reconcile any changes since this checkpoint. For new financial budget semantics, obtain explicit approval of the calculation contract before coding. Do not automatically resume #29 just because it was the former automation's top priority; flag its safety implications when planning any change.

## Release and approval invariants

Follow RELEASE_PROCESS.md for every product change: immutable versioned bundle, synchronized metadata/diagnostics, checks, GitHub CI, deployed artifact verification and browser validation when possible. Never claim full verification without evidence. Preview `main` is not the stable source `financial-autonomy-app/main`; promoting to the latter requires explicit user approval of the exact build.

## 2026-11-19 16:30 UTC — P3 CSV recurring-detector contract audited (no native code change)

- Archived WEB v18.20 bundle blob `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2`, module 329/328, inspected. Native `MoreHubView` still has the Tools parity placeholder; no native CSV parser/detector path was found in the current feature source.
- CSV import contract: maximum 2 MB; UTF-8 text with optional BOM; delimiter is semicolon when the first line contains `;`, otherwise comma; quoted fields and doubled quotes are supported; malformed/unclosed quotes, extra characters after a closing quote, duplicate/missing headers and wrong column counts are rejected.
- Required unique headers: `date, merchant, amount, currency`. Dates accept `YYYY-MM-DD` or `DD.MM.YYYY`; merchant must be non-empty and at most 120 characters; currency must be exactly RON. Only negative, non-zero amounts are expenses; positive and zero rows are ignored. Exact duplicate date/merchant/amount/currency rows are omitted and counted.
- Merchant normalization is conservative: Unicode NFKC, lowercase, trim, collapse whitespace, and strip only a trailing `ref`, `txn` or `transaction` token plus its reference value. It does not assert merchant identity.
- Detector groups by normalized merchant, requires at least two observations, and classifies intervals using weekly 7±2, monthly 30.44±5, quarterly 91.3±9, yearly 365.25±15 days (all intervals must fit the same window). Amount stability requires every value within max(100 bani, 18% of the median). Confidence is Probabil for 3+ observations, otherwise De verificat. Reasons expose occurrence count, intervals and amount variation.
- Existing-recurring duplicate check uses normalized name, same frequency, and monthly-equivalent amount tolerance max(100 bani, 18%). If a duplicate is found, the UI does not offer classification buttons; it asks the user to inspect existing commitments. Otherwise the user explicitly chooses subscription/donation/other recurring expense or ignores the candidate.
- Explicit classification creates only a recurring commitment with status active, coveredByBudget false, next payment from the last observed date/frequency and history action `Clasificare CSV confirmată`. It does not create a transaction or change balances.
- **NEXT after build77 CI verification:** port this as a local-only fileImporter + pure parser/detector engine; add deterministic tests for delimiters, quoting, malformed rows, duplicate rows, amount parsing, interval tolerances, amount stability, merchant normalization and existing-commitment collisions. Classification must call the recurring store only after an explicit user choice. No backend, telemetry or automatic transaction posting.

On 2026-10-25, 00:30Z and 01:30Z both map to local 03:30. Notification triggers now use Gregorian UTC DateComponents with UTC timezone, preserving the exact persisted instant. Added regression test testReminderTriggerComponentsDisambiguateBucharestDSTFallback. App commits [ce613d9](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/ce613d9429e39e0e52db44802682d6323fa2a4da) and [75e6f3c](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/75e6f3c35806dc2fba3a57e1d0df05ff975b2ed1).

## Native iOS — legacy Codable and ECMAScript whitespace parity (2026-11-19 23:20 UTC)

**Status:** legacy optional-field compatibility is now explicitly covered; one exact WEB v18.20 CSV/subscription whitespace mismatch was corrected on the iOS feature branch. Focused Swift/Node harnesses passed. Native Xcode build, XCTest, Simulator and visual checks remain unverified.

- Startup preview main was `c58c1becfd4142557bd08f07aa0f241fa4e07a7b`; handoff was READY with no lease. Claimed a finite lease in preview commit `6edb067c24547dd24638d8e04dbb9a66abce2c03`, expires 2026-11-19 23:45 UTC.
- Startup app feature HEAD `b8e45835ca89c7780ff674a2cfff2611f415ab1d`; app stable `main` `a9b30be147abecea45a63bf7dd8d0a3eb3370366`. Current feature HEAD `aad3fceceb184911c55d26ffb16dfd5369f43b88`; compare: **389 ahead / 0 behind**. App `main` remains unchanged.
- Legacy Codable audit: pre-build28 `WebImportedMonthPlan.budgets` and `contributionMinor`, pre-build49 `LedgerSnapshot.appearance`, and pre-build53 `WebImportMetadata.reviews` are optional and decode as absent. `BudgetSettings` explicitly defaults to weekly/50,000 and uses the old window as `budgetID`; `Operation` defaults absent `voided`/`budgeted` to false. No production migration defect was found in these fields.
- Added legacy regression coverage in `LegacyOperationDecodingTests.swift`: absent build28/49/53 fields, nil-vs-empty preservation, validator acceptance, old `BudgetSettings` fallback and missing `Operation.voided`. App commits `756cd3d198a9486b720e12f1752bb3bdda29c065` and fixture correction `5e39bd4f9f713f00f8f10e78d37778bf6fc6934b`.
- Archived WEB v18.20 bundle `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2`, modules 204/329, use ECMAScript `String.trim()` and `/\s/g`. Swift `Character.isWhitespace`/`CharacterSet.whitespacesAndNewlines` differ: Swift treats U+0085 and U+200B as whitespace but does not treat U+FEFF as whitespace; ECMAScript does the opposite. This caused real acceptance mismatches for amount parsing, CSV field trimming, text after closing quotes and merchant normalization.
- Added `WebWhitespace` with the exact ECMAScript WhiteSpace + LineTerminator scalar set and applied it to CSV parsing, CSV merchant normalization and subscription amount parsing. Commits: `261b83db827b154d873a49917174f83fb3e6188e` and `bd664ba0d9e5052dbbe4d789f53fa6253c9f9f46`.
- Added CSV and subscription regression assertions for U+FEFF acceptance and U+0085/U+200B rejection, including post-quote handling and merchant normalization. Commits: `16057201e529c25958bca0941d9ba743ae96387b` and `aad3fceceb184911c55d26ffb16dfd5369f43b88`.
- Executed local Swift 6.2.1 harness: parser/amount/merchant/quoted-field cases passed. Executed Node WEB-reference harness against module 204 whitespace normalization and module 329 merchant normalization: same cases passed. These are focused harnesses, **not XCTest or an iOS app build**.

No exact push-run ID/job/runner result is exposed. Do not infer CI pass/failure and do not retry old zero-runner attempts.

Security scope is released with `defer` after successful acquisition.

WEB picker uses `accept=".csv"`; parser detects delimiter from the first line split by `/\\r?\\n/` and iterates CR/LF separately, skipping the LF after CR.
- Concrete mismatches: native `.fileImporter` allowed both `.commaSeparatedText` and `.plainText` (broader than WEB's .csv-only filter); Swift `Array(String)` groups CRLF into one extended grapheme `Character`, so the parser's separate CR/LF comparisons did not split CRLF records. The same Character iteration also made first-line delimiter detection differ for CRLF files.

- Focused Swift 6.2.1 extracted parser-loop harness: **5 checks passed** (CRLF record boundaries, delimiter detection limited to header line, CR-only records, quoted CRLF preservation, LF records). Verified that Swift Character groups CRLF into one Character. Node reference harness using the archived WEB parser algorithm: **4 cases passed**. These are focused harnesses, not XCTest or an Xcode app build.
- Added five XCTest methods to source; the new XCTest methods, existing suite, UI tests, XcodeGen, Simulator and actual document-picker interaction have **not** been executed. `xcodebuild` is unavailable on this Linux host.

### Formula reconciliation

- Confirmed structural matches: `WebDashboardSpaceEngine` mirrors WEB module 209's owned-money / remaining-budget Space expression; `WebDailyPaceEngine.dailyBudget` mirrors module 221's included-Primary per-window daily allowance; `WebDailyPaceEngine.coverage` mirrors module 220's next-income horizon and floor safe-daily calculation; `WebAutonomyEngine` mirrors module 223's configured monthly-cost components, prorated daily budget, remainder distribution, exposed inflation and first-uncovered-day stop.
- Confirmed parity blockers: current-month `paidMonths` is preserved by the importer but ignored by both native obligation engines; daily Coverage/Spațiu does not suppress exhausted finite-installment debts as WEB module 210 does; native Autonomie has only partial installment handling and omits the WEB paid-month marker; debt `closedAt` is used by WEB module 210 but is absent from `WebImportedDebt` and rejected by the strict native parser. Refinance suppression also needs a dedicated cross-platform fixture. An additional unknown-fixed-date scope difference remains unclassified pending a representative fixture.
- Example for the paid-month contract: with 50,000 minor units of owned cash, current-month obligations of 1,500 + 10,000 + 500 all marked paid (no matching operations), and a six-day horizon, WEB's remaining obligations are 0 and safe pace is 8,333 minor/day; native currently retains 12,000 minor of obligations and computes 6,333 minor/day. Space differs by the same 12,000 minor before the remaining-budget reservation.
- No formula was changed. The exact proposed contract and source references are recorded in app commit `91d09d3859d84205e219bdfc14bb511369c36796`, `NativeiOS/PARITY_CHECKLIST.md`, Build19.

### Read-only formula reconciliation against archived WEB v18.20

Reference: `_expo/static/js/web/index-v01820-history-back-navigation-20261014.js`, blob `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2`. WEB module 209/210 owns `obligationsFor`; module 220 uses it for Coverage; module 223 uses it for Autonomie.

- Core expressions remain structurally aligned: Space's owned-money minus obligations minus non-negative remaining budget; Primary daily-budget inclusion and per-window clamp; Coverage's inclusive next-income horizon and floor safe-daily calculation; Autonomie's configured monthly-cost components, prorated daily budget, remainder distribution, exposed inflation and first-uncovered-day stop. These are source-level matches, not numeric XCTest proof.
- **Confirmed gap — current-month paid markers:** WEB skips a current-month fixed expense, scheduled debt or recurring obligation when `paidMonths[month]` contains its ID. Native `WebDailyPaceEngine.obligations` and `WebAutonomyEngine.obligations` subtract recorded operations but do not consult `WebImportMetadata.paidMonths`. This affects Coverage, Space (through `currentMonthObligationsMinor`) and Autonomie.
- **Confirmed gap — finite installments:** WEB's no-schedule debt branch uses configured installment count, historical installment count, the current-month paid marker and current-month installment transactions. Native Autonomie has partial installment-count/current-transaction logic but omits the paid-month marker; native DailyPace/Coverage/Space does not apply the finite installment limit.
- **Closed debt predicate:** WEB explicitly suppresses debt obligations when `closedAt <= asOfDay`. The native imported debt shape does not retain a `closedAt` field; strict import currently rejects this state rather than silently discarding it.
- **Refinance settlement:** WEB explicitly suppresses a debt after a qualifying non-voided refinance payment fully settles it by the as-of date. Native balance derivation may cover common loan cases, but parity for later positive observations and revolving products has not been proven; a cross-platform fixture is required.
- No formulas, balances, transaction semantics or imported debt schema were changed. These changes remain blocked until the user explicitly approves the exact v18.20 obligation contract recorded in `NativeiOS/PARITY_CHECKLIST.md` Build19 and required by AGENTS.md / DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md.

### Exact next action

1. Continue a non-financial WEB v18.20 import/backup audit with populated fixtures, focusing on remaining nested metadata and full legacy snapshot round-trips; add only evidence-backed lossless-restore regressions and preserve strict rejection of unsupported fields.
2. Recheck live refs and exact-HEAD Native iOS CI next run; do not retry run `44762409132` while it has zero runner/steps/logs/artifacts.
3. At the first macOS runner that executes steps, run XcodeGen, Simulator build, the full unit/UI suite, inspect the exact `.xcresult`, then exercise the Files/document-picker flows and compare populated Light/Dark screens against archived WEB v18.20.
4. Implement the paid-month, finite-installment, closed-debt and refinance obligation contract only after explicit user approval and with shared WEB/native reference fixtures.

No production formula change, no `Models.swift` or `LedgerStore.swift` rewrite, no app/main modification or merge. Privacy remains local-only; no backend, mandatory account, bank sync or telemetry. Available credit remains excluded from owned money. No 100% parity claim.

It deliberately rejects surrounding whitespace and non-standard ±HH offsets, both rejected by the Node 22.16.0 reference. The raw reminder string is not rewritten.
- WebBackupV18_20 validation, SubscriptionEngine validation/display and local notification scheduling now share the parser. The existing UTC UNCalendarNotificationTrigger components remain unchanged. The encrypted recurring lifecycle round-trip fixture uses a non-UTC offset and asserts the original text survives.

- App commits:
  - [`c488a10899be4bd9d5ad1e1f71634b8c8d89a21c`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/c488a10899be4bd9d5ad1e1f71634b8c8d89a21c): native magic-header dispatch, distinct authentication/format/invalid-ledger errors, wrong-password and WEB fallback regressions, BackupFormat documentation.

- Concrete defect: `BackupRestorePreparation.prepare` previously used `try?` for native open and then always tried the WEB JSON decoder. A native `FAiOSBK1` backup with a wrong password or authentication failure therefore surfaced as “not a compatible WEB backup”, masking the actual failure. It now selects the native decoder by its magic header and propagates native failures. Invalid combined-box shape maps to `format`, AES-GCM authentication failure maps to `authentication`, JSON decode failure maps to `invalidLedger`, and PBKDF derivation errors remain unmasked. Native v1 header/KDF/password semantics and WEB envelope remain unchanged.
- Regression coverage: wrong native password through the preparation boundary must return `PortableBackup.Failure.authentication`; the archived encrypted WEB v18.20 fixture must still route through the WEB decoder when native magic is absent. The full ledger remains unchanged until the existing explicit restore confirmation.
- Independent Files read audit: Apple documents `FileHandle.read(upToCount:)` for a file as returning the requested bytes or the remaining bytes through EOF; no speculative chunk-reader change was made. This is API-contract/source review only, not actual iOS Files-provider verification: https://developer.apple.com/documentation/foundation/filehandle/read%28uptocount%3A%29
- Executed locally with Swift 6.2.1: isolated dispatch harness **PASS** (native authentication error preserved; WEB fallback works; denied security scope stops before read); `swiftc -parse` of the changed PortableBackup error-mapping snippet **PASS**. Harness uses CryptoKit/WEB decoder stubs; these are not app-target XCTest or a cryptographic integration test.

- Module 309 exports `financial-autonomy.backup.json` as `application/json`.
- Module 311 creates a JSON `FinancialAutonomy` v1 envelope: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 / 600,000 iterations, NFKC-normalized password, 16-byte salt, 12-byte nonce, hex ciphertext and AAD `FinancialAutonomy/backup/1`. WEB restore rejects strings longer than exactly 8,000,000 characters; the envelope is ASCII, so its byte limit is 8,000,000.

### App changes
- Commit [`82dac9d1066c7a93faae7fefad405838e5d8788d`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/82dac9d1066c7a93faae7fefad405838e5d8788d) updates seven files on the feature branch only:

  - `EncryptedBackupDocument.readableContentTypes` keeps the native type, generic `.data` legacy fallback and WEB `.json`; `writableContentTypes` now advertises only the native backup type. `fileExporter` uses that custom type; importer still accepts all three formats.
  - Backup UI now states explicitly that native v1 export is available, WEB v18.20 JSON is import-only, and a WEB-compatible export is not implemented.
  - Native WEB importer limit changed from 8 MiB (8,388,608 bytes) to the archived WEB contract's exact 8,000,000 bytes; oversized input now produces a dedicated `tooLarge` error instead of a misleading incompatible-format error.
  - Added source regressions for document type separation and the WEB size boundary; updated BackupFormat and PARITY_CHECKLIST.
- Native `FAiOSBK1` v1 binary envelope, 32 MiB limit, PBKDF2 inputs, password handling, restore confirmation, Models.swift, LedgerStore.swift and all financial formulas are unchanged.
- **Remaining confirmed gap:** the native app still has no native-to-WEB v18.20 exporter. It is intentionally not synthesized by dropping native-only fields; a complete lossless field mapping/rejection contract is still required.

## Native iOS checkpoint — WEB v18.20 year-zero date-only validator matrix (2026-11-22T11:53:00Z)

- Preview main at audit start: `f75bb84b08da0e647f2da23bf29fa14b315852f4` (the active handoff lease commit). App feature before this audit: `665c9c007e772a94f28e8511fb9a83e27d06cbe4`; app stable main `a9b30be147abecea45a63bf7dd8d0a3eb3370366` unchanged.

Previous code/test commit remains `93e7f56a3eab91712ee9026a9c7d7366143a1534`; no production Swift, Models, LedgerStore, financial formula, persisted user data, app/main or privacy architecture changed.
- Executed the actual archived WEB v18.20 bundle blob `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2` Metro module 205 `validateState` and dependencies in an isolated V8 module loader. The validator accepted `0000-02-29` for: root `started`/ `rolesEffectiveFrom`; account and debt opening dates; debt schedule dates; transaction dates; goal deadlines; plan-step due dates; month-plan primary/additional budget-window dates; recurring next-payment/history/end dates; operation audit dates; balance-check dates and check-audit before-image dates; and legacy v1 spending dates. Invalid `0000-02-30` was rejected for fields using the actual date validator.
- Month keys `monthPlans.month`, `reviews`, and `paidMonths` accept `0000-02` by four-digit-year/month regex. These are month keys, not full dates. Native restore has a downstream gap: `SnapshotValidator` validates month-plan months with `RecurrenceCalendar.dueDate`, and `ScheduledDueEngine` validates paid-month keys with the same calendar, whose parser rejects year zero. Review keys remain regex-backed.
- Archived WEB validation limitations: module 205 checks `nameChanges` only as an array and does not validate row dates; module 207 checks top-level `checkAudit[].date` only by `YYYY-MM-DD` shape. A malformed `0000-02-30` was accepted in those two weakly validated positions; the check-audit before-image date is independently calendar-validated. A reminder timestamp `0000-02-29T12:00:00.000Z` was accepted, but it is a timestamp rather than a date-only field.
- Native source inspection: `WebBackupV18_20.validDay` uses strict Gregorian UTC `DateFormatter`; `RecurrenceCalendar.parse` explicitly limits years to `1...9999`. Import converts transaction and balance-check days to Foundation `Date`; `SnapshotValidator`, `ScheduledDueEngine`, and the loan/financial calendar paths revalidate dates during restore and replay. Swift 6.2.1 Linux probes rejected year-zero DateFormatter input; Foundation Calendar mapped `DateComponents(year: 0)` to year 1, and an era-aware 1-BCE date read without `.era` also returns year 1.
- Decision: no runtime change. Parser-only acceptance would fail at Date conversion, snapshot validation, schedule validation or replay and could serialize year zero as year one. A safe fix needs a complete backup-scoped proleptic-Gregorian date-only contract across all imported date fields, Date-backed operations/observations, validators, schedule/loan consumers, formatting and encrypted-backup round trips. Do not widen global `RecurrenceCalendar` or coerce year zero into Foundation `Date`.

Combined contexts for the later docs-only HEAD `cc4c761` are empty. No retry. Swift 6.2.1 Linux is available; `xcodebuild` and `xcodegen` are absent. No Apple build, app XCTest/UI, Simulator, Files restore, .xcresult or visual comparison.

- Reconciled the stale 43387855 handoff against two later feature commits: `d9987274762a9eb6dffb55c1535ed33b949b3c9c` (date presentation, history/budget date resolution, legacy WEB v4 provenance backfill and encrypted-backup tests) and `ca26e8dbaa2e0cec8f21aae1375878922461f3ab` (skip unnecessary provenance scans and fail closed on invalid dates).
- New app commit: `e1462b866d710f3586698bb709aa8efb59df7feb` — `fix: validate source-day provenance during legacy restore`. Legacy backfill now restores a marker only when the persisted Foundation Date exactly matches the verified WEB v4 UTC-Gregorian noon instant for that source day. ## Native iOS checkpoint — Coverage expense dayKnown parity (2026-11-23T02:22Z)

- Continued from the first unfinished item: Apple-target build/XCTest remains blocked before runner allocation, so an independent source-level Coverage calculation audit was performed. Archived WEB v18.20 bundle blob: be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2.
- WEB v18.20 Coverage explicitly checks every top-level state.expenses entry for dayKnown === false before calculating obligations, regardless of the current coverage horizon. Native previously checked only monthPlans[].expenses within the horizon. This could both miss an unknown top-level expense preset and incorrectly block on a month-plan row when the WEB top-level preset is known.
- App commit [cb553dc2](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/cb553dc236d53b6974908bde34329adfc88e223e) changes WebDailyPaceEngine to check metadata.expenseTemplates globally, matching the archived WEB condition, and adds two regressions in NativeiOS/Tests/WebBudgetWindowEngineTests.swift: unknown top-level preset blocks even with empty month-plan rows; unknown month-plan row does not override a known top-level WEB preset.
- Source evidence: WEB module 220 uses t.expenses.some(n => n.dayKnown === false); native WebV18_20StateMapper.mapTemplates projects state.expenses into metadata.expenseTemplates, while monthPlans[].expenses are a separate collection. Existing model types preserve dayKnown independently for both collections.
- Additional Coverage cash-source audit: WEB availableMoney uses accountBalance/checkedBalance anchored at the last non-voided balance check plus unabsorbed operations through the current day. Native LedgerStore updates MoneyAccount.balanceMinor atomically for operations, excludes accounts whose operations are absorbed by linked observations, and sets balanceMinor to the observed real balance on observeBalance; correction/void paths adjust the current balance and audit. Coverage filters checking/savings/cash and availableForSpending != false, matching WEB available !== false for the native-owned account kinds. Closed accounts require zero balance. No evidence of a current-day cash-source mismatch was found; no formula change was made.

## Native iOS checkpoint — dailyBudget legacy-role and rounding parity (2026-11-23T03:16:22.905Z)

- Startup re-read the current preview-main `AGENTS.md`, `PROJECT_STATE.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION_QUEUE.md`, `DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md`, and `NATIVE_WORK_HANDOFF.md`. Handoff was READY_FOR_NEXT_RUN with no lease. Current preview main at lease claim: 3a1af4c05f793be3fcbbb16896060941d9a27776; app feature HEAD at startup: cb553dc236d53b6974908bde34329adfc88e223e; app stable main: a9b30be147abecea45a63bf7dd8d0a3eb3370366. Claimed a finite documentation lease in preview commit 1bde48f042a6a82fcd432dd8ea2c41459c113419, expiring 2026-11-23T03:55:00Z.
- Archived target remains WEB v18.20 bundle `_expo/static/js/web/index-v01820-history-back-navigation-20261014.js`, blob `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2`. Active preview main is WEB 18.74 and was not substituted as the parity target.
- Source comparison of archived modules 206, 219 and 220 found a concrete backward-compatibility mismatch. WEB `roleOf` treats an expense with a categoryId as budgeted when `financialRole` is absent. Native `Operation.financialRole` is optional; its Codable decoder preserves nil while retaining `budgetID`. The v18.20 mapper already applies the same budgetID fallback when exporting. However, native `WebBudgetWindowEngine.spentMinor` and `WebDailyPaceEngine.dailyBudget` previously required explicit `.budgetedExpense`, so old category-linked records could be omitted from budget spend, inflate remaining budget/daily allowance, be omitted from outside-pace, and be incorrectly counted as outside-budget spend.
- App commits on `financial-autonomy-app:feat/sync-preview-v18-20-ios`:
  - [b2a9b8b4](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/b2a9b8b441ad3be54f6c799301a2c9d11482b1a1) adds `BudgetAllocationEngine.isBudgetedExpense`, preserving explicit role precedence and matching WEB's category/budget-ID fallback only when role is absent.
  - [d8dd9763](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/d8dd9763a580217e9750bd4bf648bac358bb622e) uses the helper in budget-window historical spend.
  - [d5875d19](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/d5875d195b763019bd2e9bff3a7ae0dac67ed0b5) uses the helper for today's budgeted transactions, outside-pace and outside-budget classification.
  - [f171f4b4](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/f171f4b4492f6a077a5b000f7536c3e8592c253c) adds regressions for legacy role fallback, excluded pace budgets, outside-budget classification and positive half-up rounding.
- The regression scenario expects historical/current budgeted spend 31,500 against a 31,000 monthly limit, current included-budget spend 1,000, excluded-pace spend 500, unbudgeted spend 250 and a safe daily allowance of 500. A separate April 2026 case checks 15,015 / 30 = 500.5 rounds to 501, matching WEB `Math.round`. ## Native iOS checkpoint — 2026-11-23T09:25Z — Autonomy disclosure and Budget visual parity

- Startup re-read preview-main `AGENTS.md`, `PROJECT_STATE.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION_QUEUE.md`, `DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md` and `NATIVE_WORK_HANDOFF.md`. Handoff was `READY_FOR_NEXT_RUN` with no lease. - Archived WEB v18.20 reference remains `_expo/static/js/web/index-v01820-history-back-navigation-20261014.js`, blob `be453db2699273a194543de4532db6ea4b2e1cf2`. Module 303 `AutonomyHome` and module 218 `BudgetDashboard` were inspected directly; no active preview bundle or financial engine was changed.

### AutonomyHome — source-confirmed disclosure parity

- WEB module 303 renders the Autonomy card as a non-interactive hero container. Only a trailing 44×36 disclosure button toggles the calculation; its accessibility labels are `Vezi calculul` / `Ascunde calculul`. Expanded content sits in a separate `budgetSurface` panel with radius 18, padding 18 and gap 12.
- Native previously made the entire hero card a button and placed the calculation rows directly on the hero surface. App commit [`1dfbb55e`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/1dfbb55eee15ddc281a602d6f45c9b40b4c5a72c) separates the card from its disclosure, adds the WEB panel and matches the heading/number/unit/summary geometry and row typography. App commit [`15bc1616`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/15bc1616a9e5efc64de513e6772522be35c8797b) locks the 25pt detail-row line box.
- App commits [`5a213a1f`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/5a213a1f8a2972e607d88a2f0b74dda1e3b31bfc), [`bff4aa72`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/bff4aa722979e398b047168c1735ddade656c194), and [`de75d308`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/de75d308577cf55b6f9235d41ab3506564ee0b44) add Light/Dark UI assertions and exact token regressions, including the non-button card root, arrow target size, panel content, colors and line-height.

### BudgetDashboard — source-confirmed visual parity

- WEB module 218 uses a 14pt/20 label and 17pt/25 value for daily rows, an accent-colored 7pt/radius-5 daily bar, 13pt/21 window names, 14pt/20 window values, and window progress colors mapped to semantic danger/warning/selected-accent tokens.
- Native compact Dashboard rows previously used caption/system red-orange colors and the system accent. App commit [`a70931eb`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/a70931eb7e1476923162ed9eaeae4e15006e277f) exposes exact WEB semantic palette tokens; [`98392373`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/98392373201478d940ac6a2df9811ad902ac391d) aligns compact row typography and progress colors; [`8b63b516`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/8b63b516f1d0bd99b3c6b635e7b6a28771b113d6) applies semantic danger/warning to unavailable and uncovered states; [`0dd0967f`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/0dd0967f3bd3ce7c7750500085de38db870b9c06) preserves the non-compact Budget view's existing primary-text behavior. App commit [`bd8c94e9`](https://github.com/nikopol000/financial-autonomy-app/commit/bd8c94e97ed6894cdc08c2230a1f9eec2818bf1b) adds Light/Dark palette assertions for Cyan/Ocean/Iris and WEB danger/warning/text tokens.
- The existing `WebBudgetWindowsView` and `WebBudgetWindowEngine` daily allowance, coverage, budget windows and pinned-window behavior were left unchanged. This stage changes presentation only; no budget formula was altered.

### MoneyHome audit — financial work intentionally not changed

- WEB module 303 `MoneyHome` displays five expandable groups (Disponibil, Economii, Datorez, Credit and the unexplained-balance reconciliation card). Native `DashboardView.moneyModule` currently displays only Disponibil propriu and Credit disponibil; the remaining content is a confirmed functional/visual parity gap.
- WEB filters the Disponibil accounts with `available !== false`. Native `LedgerSafeTotals.ownedMoneyMinor` currently sums checking/savings/cash accounts without filtering `availableForSpending != false`; `WebDailyPaceEngine.coverage` already applies that filter. This is a source-confirmed financial semantic discrepancy. Per `AGENTS.md` and `DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md`, no formula/meaning change was made without explicit approval and a reviewed testable proposal. Credit remains excluded from owned money.
